Kingdom of the Fairies · Production art

Prepared film-edition assets with unchanged native originals and submitted prompts. Legacy artwork is retained. Delivery does not implement new story chapters.

Requests and delivery status · Delivery notes · Manifest

The apotheosis

apotheosis

2560 × 1440 · full frame

Native original · Generation record

Submitted prompt

From references/film-planning/originals/concept-apotheosis.png (attach it) without figures: clouds parting onto a marble colonnade in tiers of light, gilded and starry. A painted theatre backcloth seen square-on, full bleed to every edge, scene painting in the manner of Georges Méliès' 1903 film Le Royaume des fées: early-1900s stencil-tinted colour (sepia-grey line and tone with translucent washes of plum #2a1424, rose pink #f3c6dc, magenta #e0127a, moon gold #d9b34a, coral red #c8321e, grotto teal #5f8c88 and azure #6f9fc8 laid slightly out of register); no lettering; faint film grain; no figures unless the prompt asks for them. Generate exactly one production theatre set, no collage, labels, border, proscenium or figures beyond explicitly requested set ornaments. Canvas aspect ratio 2560:1440, intended delivery 2560 x 1440 pixels. Full-bleed painted background to all four edges; no transparency or frame. Asset apotheosis.

armour-helmet, tinted after the film

armour-helmet-tinted

128 × 128 · bottom centre

Native original · Generation record

Submitted prompt

Redraw armour-helmet.webp (attach it) in the look of the 1903 film (attach references/expanded-world/source-frames/frame-00m26s.jpg, frame-04m54s.jpg and frame-05m51s.jpg). Keep exactly the same subject, pose, framing, scale, foot position and canvas, so it lays over the original. A stage armour piece, grey metal, the plume a rose wash. Render it like a frame of a hand stencil-coloured 1903 Méliès print, not an illustration: a soft-focus black-and-white photograph of a real stage performer in costume (silvery greys, deep blacks, gentle film grain and a little halation), with only a few flat, translucent aniline dye washes laid over by stencil (sea-green teal, orange-yellow, rose and coral red, pale sky blue, magenta), each bleeding slightly past its edges and a little out of register, and large areas left untinted grey; a simple theatre costume of cloth, tights, wig and painted card props with little fine detail and no gold filigree; flat frontal stage lighting; no ink outlines and no glossy finish. Isolated on a transparent background (or flat pure magenta #FF00FF if transparency is unavailable). Image 1 is the edit target, images 2–4 are film-style references only. Keep the exact subject, pose, prop positions, facing direction and full silhouette of image 1. Output one isolated sprite on a genuinely transparent canvas with aspect ratio 128:128, intended delivery 128 x 128. Preserve correct limb counts and anatomical hand grips for any performer; no extra arms, hands, fingers or detached props.

The plumed helmet (pickup)

armour-helmet

128 × 128 · bottom centre

Native original · Generation record

Submitted prompt

The prince's silver plumed helmet with its magenta plume, from char-bel-azor.png (attach it), resting on its own, seen from the side. Flat painted toy-theatre cut-out seen square-on, gouache on card with a faint pale cut edge, in the manner of Georges Méliès' 1903 film Le Royaume des fées: early-1900s stencil-tinted colour (sepia-grey line and tone with translucent washes of plum #2a1424, rose pink #f3c6dc, magenta #e0127a, moon gold #d9b34a, coral red #c8321e, grotto teal #5f8c88 and azure #6f9fc8 laid slightly out of register); no lettering; no cast shadow; isolated on a transparent background (or flat pure magenta #FF00FF if transparency is unavailable). Generate exactly one asset, no collage or labels. True transparent background. Intended delivery 128 x 128 pixels. Match the reference character, costume, palette and painted card edges. Preserve the complete silhouette.

The blue attendant, tumbling (tinted)

attendant-blue-tumble-tinted

300 × 300 · bottom centre

Native original · Generation record

Submitted prompt

The courtier exactly as in attendant-blue-tinted.webp (attach it): the same face, gown, colours and scale (her standing figure fills a 420 px canvas), tumbling head over heels as she is swept into the whale's throat, gown and veil streaming, arms flung out. Nothing but the figure: no water or shadow. Used for: Act III, swept into the whale beside the prince (drawn now as her standing figure spun round). Render it like a frame of a hand stencil-coloured 1903 Méliès print, not an illustration: a soft-focus black-and-white photograph of a real stage performer in costume (silvery greys, deep blacks, gentle film grain and a little halation), with only a few flat, translucent aniline dye washes laid over by stencil (sea-green teal, orange-yellow, rose and coral red, pale sky blue, magenta), each bleeding slightly past its edges and a little out of register, and large areas left untinted grey; a simple theatre costume of cloth, tights, wig and painted card props with little fine detail and no gold filigree; flat frontal stage lighting; no ink outlines and no glossy finish. Isolated on a transparent background (or flat pure magenta #FF00FF if transparency is unavailable). Generate one asset only, no collage or labels. True transparent background. Intended delivery [300, 300] px. Keep the complete silhouette inside the canvas. Image 1 is the exact identity, costume, palette and style reference. Repose that same performer as specified; exactly two arms and two legs per person, anatomically coherent hands, no extra limbs. If hands must plant on the ground or grip a ledge, sword stays SHEATHED at hip and shield is secured on back. No floating weapon or duplicated equipment. Preserve full silhouette and match requested canvas aspect ratio. Retain true transparency.

Aurora gliding, frame A (tinted)

aurora-glide-a-tinted

300 × 420 · bottom centre

Native original · Generation record

Submitted prompt

the fairy Aurora as in references/film-planning/originals/char-aurora.png (attach it): white and moon-gold gauze with rose and plum veils, a starred diadem and a star-tipped wand, exactly as in aurora-stand-tinted.webp (attach it): the same face, gown, veils, diadem, colours and scale (her figure fills the same height). Gliding through the air facing LEFT, a fairy's flight rather than a walk: feet together and pointed just clear of the ground, her body leaning slightly forward into the glide, veils and gown streaming out BEHIND her to the right, wand held forward and low with its star glowing. Frame A: veils lifted high behind her. The lowest point of the figure (her pointed toes) on the bottom edge; centred on the canvas. Nothing but the figure: no sparkle trail, ground or shadow (the game adds the sparkle). Render it like a frame of a hand stencil-coloured 1903 Méliès print, not an illustration: a soft-focus black-and-white photograph of a real stage performer in costume (silvery greys, deep blacks, gentle film grain and a little halation), with only a few flat, translucent aniline dye washes laid over by stencil (sea-green teal, orange-yellow, rose and coral red, pale sky blue, magenta), each bleeding slightly past its edges and a little out of register, and large areas left untinted grey; a simple theatre costume of cloth, tights, wig and painted card props with little fine detail and no gold filigree; flat frontal stage lighting; no ink outlines and no glossy finish. Isolated on a transparent background (or flat pure magenta #FF00FF if transparency is unavailable). Generate exactly one complete figure, no collage, labels or other figures. True transparent background. Preserve reference identity and proportions. Leave a tiny safe margin so no extremity is clipped; delivery fitting will align the bottom edge.
